Partial unique index on `lower(display_name)` where not deactivated. |\n| `articles` | `id` identity \u00b7 `google_article_id` unique \u00b7 `title` \u00b7 `snippet` \u00b7 `outlet_name` \u00b7 `outlet_url` \u00b7 `google_url` \u00b7 `publisher_url` null \u00b7 `published_at` \u00b7 `language` \u00b7 `edition` \u00b7 `first_fetched_at` |\n| `candidates` | `id` \u00b7 `article_id` FK \u00b7 `company_id` FK \u00b7 unique (`article_id`, `company_id`) \u00b7 `fetched_in_run_id` \u00b7 `relevance` enum `pending` / `relevant` / `rejected` \u00b7 `relevance_method` enum `llm` / `name_absent` \u00b7 `relevance_reason` \u00b7 `sentiment` enum null \u00b7 `sentiment_reason` \u00b7 `confirmed_in_run_id` null \u00b7 `confirmed_at` null \u00b7 classification timestamps. **A Mention is a candidate with `relevance = 'relevant'`.** |\n| `runs` | `id` \u00b7 `type` enum `backfill` / `daily_check` \u00b7 `status` enum `queued` / `running` / `completed` / `completed_with_errors` / `failed` / `interrupted` \u00b7 `params` jsonb (`until`, `companyIds`, `reprocess`) \u00b7 `trigger` enum `dashboard` / `schedule` \u00b7 `progress` jsonb \u00b7 `error` \u00b7 timestamps. Partial unique index allowing one row with status in (`queued`, `running`). |\n| `run_company_errors` | `run_id` \u00b7 `company_id` \u00b7 `stage` \u00b7 `message` |\n| `alert_digests` | `id` \u00b7 `run_id` unique \u00b7 `created_at` \u00b7 `acknowledged_at` null |\n| `alert_digest_items` | `digest_id` \u00b7 `candidate_id` |\n| `collector_heartbeat` | single row: `last_seen_at` \u00b7 `state` (`idle` / `importing` / `running`) \u00b7 `ollama_ok` \u00b7 `ollama_model` \u00b7 `detail` |\n\n## Ports (interfaces + injection tokens, defined in #4)\n\n| Port | Method(s) \u2014 domain terms | Implementations |\n|---|---|---|\n| `NewsSource` | `findCandidates(company: CompanyProfile, window: {from, to}, edition): Promise<FoundArticle[]>`; errors `NewsSourceUnavailable` | Google News RSS (#5); in-memory with recorded fixtures |\n| `RelevanceClassifier` | `judge(company, article): Promise<RelevanceVerdict>`; error `ClassifierUnavailable`, `ClassifierOutputInvalid` | Ollama (#6) |\n| `SentimentClassifier` | `classify(company, article): Promise<SentimentVerdict>` | Ollama (#6) |\n| `AmbiguityTriage` | `assess(name): Promise<{ambiguous, reason}>` | Ollama (#6) |\n| `TrackedCompanyRepository` | list / get / create / update / setStatus | Postgres (#7) |\n| `RunQueue` | `enqueue(request)` (throws `RunAlreadyActive`), `claimNext()`, `reportProgress`, `finish` | Postgres (#8) |\n| `RunExecutor` | `execute(run, progress): Promise<RunOutcome>` \u2014 one per Run type | Backfill, Daily Check (#9) |\n| `AlertNotifier` | `notify(digest): Promise<void>` | DB + log + `data/alerts/<date>.json` (#10) |\n| `DataExporter` | `exportAll(): Promise<void>` | `data/` JSON writer (#12) |\n\nEvery port ships its in-memory implementation with its first real one; test contents are recorded\nfrom real responses (ADR-006), never hand-written.\n\n## Configuration keys (all added in #4)\n\n`PORT=8000` \u00b7 `DATABASE_URL` \u00b7 `OLLAMA_BASE_URL=http://localhost:11434` \u00b7 `OLLAMA_MODEL=qwen2.5:7b` \u00b7\n`OLLAMA_NUM_PARALLEL=2` \u00b7 `NEWS_EDITIONS=en-US,he-IL` \u00b7 `DAILY_CHECK_SCHEDULE_ENABLED=true` \u00b7\n`DAILY_CHECK_CRON=0 7 * * *` \u00b7 `TZ=Asia/Jerusalem` \u00b7 `NEW_MENTION_MAX_AGE_DAYS=7` \u00b7\n`OLLAMA_FAILURE_THRESHOLD=5` \u00b7 `RUN_POLL_INTERVAL_MS=3000` \u00b7 `DATA_EXPORT_DIR=../data` \u00b7\n`SEED_LIST_PATH=../docs/ourcrowd_companies.txt` \u00b7 `MAX_CANDIDATES_PER_COMPANY` (unset = no cap)\n\n## HTTP API contract (`/api` prefix; `GET /health` unprefixed)\n\nThe frontend codes against this contract from day one; its TypeScript mirror lives in\n`frontend/src/types.ts` (#3). `window` is `rolling90` (default) or a quarter like `2026-Q3`.\n\n| Method & path | Purpose | Notes |\n|---|---|---|\n| `GET /api/summary?window=` | Summary strip: companies per Mention Status, Mentions in window, sentiment split, negative count | |\n| `GET /api/companies?window=&status=&hasNegatives=&q=&sort=` | Overview table rows: id, displayName, mentionStatus, lastMentionAt, mentionCount, capped, sentiment split, latestHeadline | default sort: negatives in window desc, then recency |\n| `GET /api/companies/:id?window=` | Detail: profile, Mention Status, weekly series by sentiment, rejection rate | |\n| `GET /api/companies/:id/candidates?window=&include=mentions\\|rejected\\|all` | Mentions (and rejected Candidates with reasons) | paginated |\n| `GET /api/admin/companies?status=&q=` | Company management list including Needs Review and deactivated | |\n| `POST /api/admin/companies` | Add a company (no Source Name) | DTO-validated |\n| `PATCH /api/admin/companies/:id` | Edit any field except `sourceName` | 400 if `sourceName` sent |\n| `POST /api/admin/companies/:id/review` | Mark reviewed \u2192 `active` | |\n| `POST /api/admin/companies/:id/needs-review` | Send back to Needs Review | |\n| `POST /api/admin/companies/:id/deactivate` | Deactivate (history kept) | |\n| `POST /api/admin/companies/:id/reprocess` | Enqueue a single-company Backfill with `reprocess: true` | 202 / 409 |\n| `POST /api/runs` `{type, until?, companyIds?}` | Enqueue Backfill or Daily Check | 202 / 409 with the active Run |\n| `GET /api/runs?limit=` \u00b7 `GET /api/runs/active` | Run history and live progress | polled every few seconds |\n| `GET /api/collector/health` | Heartbeat: online/offline, state, Ollama ok, model | |\n| `GET /api/alerts?acknowledged=false` \u00b7 `GET /api/alerts/:id` \u00b7 `POST /api/alerts/:id/acknowledge` | Alert Digests | |\n\nState-changing endpoints are plain JSON POST/PATCH; auth is deferred (BACKLOG).\n\n## Milestones, issues and dependencies\n\nIssue unit tests cover `coverageWindow.ts` and the client's error mapping.\n- [ ] Each page/panel lives in its own file so UI issues do not edit the same file.\n\n## Depends on\nNothing (contract is in the plan).\n\n## Files\n`frontend/**`.\nEOF\ncat > 04.md <<'EOF'\nPart of [IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md](../blob/main/docs/IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md) \u00b7 Milestone **M0 \u2014 Foundation** \u00b7 ADR-002, -004, -005, -008, -009, -010\n\n## Goal\nLay down every shared contract **once**, so the eight M1 issues can proceed in parallel touching only their own files: the full schema, the domain value objects, every port with its token and error classes, every config key, and empty feature modules already registered in both roots.\n\n## Scope\n- **Migration + entities** for all tables in the plan's schema section (`tracked_companies`, `articles`, `candidates`, `runs`, `run_company_errors`, `alert_digests`, `alert_digest_items`, `collector_heartbeat`), including the partial unique indexes (one active Run; unique `lower(display_name)` among non-deactivated companies; unique `source_name` when not null; unique (`article_id`,`company_id`)).\n- **`src/domain/`** (pure, fully unit-tested):\n  - `Sentiment`, `RelevanceVerdict`, `SentimentVerdict`, `CompanyProfile`, `FoundArticle`, `RunRequest`, `RunOutcome` types.\n  - `CoverageWindow`: parse `rolling90` / `YYYY-Qn`, resolve to `{from, to}` as of a given instant.\n  - `MentionStatus`: Active \u22647 d, Recent 8\u201330 d, Quiet 31\u201390 d, No coverage \u2014 from the last Mention date and \"now\".\n  - Seed-line parser: `X (formerly Y)` / `(formerly known as Y)` \u2192 alias, `(domain.tld)` \u2192 domain, `(Expansion)` \u2192 alias, legal suffixes (`Ltd.`, `Inc.`) stripped into an alias. Server validation errors shown per field.\n- Actions: **Mark reviewed**, Send to Needs Review, **Deactivate** (confirm in-page, not \\`window.confirm\\`), **Re-process** (explains that history for this company is replaced; shows 409 \"another Run is active\" nicely). After saving a profile or reviewing, offer Re-process.\n- **Add company** form (no Source Name).\n\n## Acceptance criteria\n- [ ] Component tests: source name not editable, validation errors rendered, 409 on re-process handled, add flow.\n\n## Depends on\n#3\n\n## Files\n\\`frontend/src/components/CompaniesPage.tsx\\` and components it alone uses.\nEOF\ncat > 16.md <<EOF\n$H \u00b7 ADR-004, ADR-009\n\n## Goal\nThe alert bell + Alert Digest panel, and the \\`OperationsPage\\` for Runs and collector health.\n\n$COMMON\n\n## Scope\n- \\`AlertBell\\` (replaces #3's placeholder): unacknowledged count, polled; opens a panel listing digests; each digest shows New Mentions grouped by company, negatives first, with links; **Acknowledge** button. A new digest appearing while the app is open pops a visible in-app notification.\n- \\`OperationsPage\\`:\n  - **Start Backfill** form: optional cutoff (\\`until\\`: date or \"N days ago\", default today) and optional company multi-select.\n  - **Run Daily Check now** button.\n  - Active Run progress (companies done / total, candidates classified), polled every few seconds; buttons disabled while a Run is queued/running; 409 handled.\n  - Run history (type, trigger, status incl. completed-with-errors with per-company errors, timings, counts).\n  - Collector health: online/offline, state (idle/importing/running), Ollama reachable, model.\n- Shell \"as of\" slot fed from the latest completed Run.\n\n## Acceptance criteria\n- [ ] Component tests: polling start/stop, disabled states, acknowledge flow, error rendering.\n\n## Depends on\n#3\n\n## Files\n\\`frontend/src/components/AlertBell.tsx\\`, \\`OperationsPage.tsx\\` and components they alone use.\nEOF\nH3='Part of [IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md](../blob/main/docs/IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md) \u00b7 Milestone **M3 \u2014 Delivery**'\ncat > 17.md <<EOF\n$H3 \u00b7 ADR-007, ADR-009\n\n## Goal\nClone-to-dashboard in two commands, with clear errors when a prerequisite is missing.\n\n## Scope\n- Root \\`package.json\\` (no runtime deps) with scripts calling small Node \\`.mjs\\` scripts in \\`scripts/\\`:\n  - \\`npm run setup\\`: check Docker running, Node \u226522, Ollama installed (else print \\`brew install ollama\\` / download link \u2014 never install it), \\`ollama pull \\$OLLAMA_MODEL\\` if missing, \\`npm ci\\` in \\`backend/\\` and \\`frontend/\\`.\n  - \\`npm start\\`: ensure Ollama is serving (start \\`ollama serve\\` only if the port is free), \\`docker compose up --build -d\\`, wait for \\`/health\\`, print the dashboard URL.\n  - \\`npm stop\\`: \\`docker compose down\\` (leaves Ollama alone).\n  - \\`npm run dev\\`: Postgres in compose; backend API, collector and Vite natively with reload.\n  - \\`npm run import-data\\`: run #12's import inside the api container.\n- Add the \\`frontend\\` service to \\`docker-compose.yml\\` (port 8080, depends on api).\n- Cross-platform (macOS/Linux/Windows): no bash-only constructs.\n\n## Acceptance criteria\n- [ ] From a fresh clone on macOS with Docker + Ollama: \\`npm run setup && npm start\\` \u2192 dashboard on http://localhost:8080 shows the collector importing companies.\n- [ ] Each failed prerequisite prints the exact fix and exits non-zero.\n- [ ] \\`npm start\\` twice is safe.\n\n## Depends on\n#2, #3\n\n## Files\n\\`package.json\\` (root), \\`scripts/*.mjs\\`, \\`docker-compose.yml\\` (frontend service only).\nEOF\ncat > 18.md <<EOF\n$H3 \u00b7 ADR-002, ADR-006\n\n## Goal\nEvidence for the README's \"how classification quality was validated\" section, plus measured throughput.\n\n## Scope\n- A labelled set of ~60 **real** Candidates collected by the pipeline, weighted toward ambiguous names, ~10 Hebrew: human labels for relevance and sentiment, committed under \\`backend/test/fixtures/validation/\\`.\n- \\`npm run eval:classifiers\\`: runs the real Ollama classifiers over the set and reports relevance precision/recall and sentiment accuracy + confusion matrix, and median/p95 latency per call. Writes \\`docs/validation-report.md\\`.\n- Iterate prompts in #6's files if results are poor (separate small PR referencing this issue).\n\n## Acceptance criteria\n- [ ] Report committed with numbers, the model, prompt version and date.\n- [ ] Latency figure available for the README's Backfill duration estimate.\n\n## Depends on\n#5, #6\n\n## Files\n\\`backend/test/fixtures/validation/**\\`, eval script, \\`docs/validation-report.md\\`.\nEOF\ncat > 19.md <<EOF\n$H3\n\n## Goal\nA README a stranger can follow from clone to dashboard, covering every item the brief lists.\n\n## Scope\n- What it does and how it's structured (diagram from the plan, two containers + host Ollama).\n- Setup: prerequisites, Ollama install, \\`ollama pull qwen2.5:7b\\`, env vars table (all config keys with defaults).\n- Exact commands: \\`npm run setup\\`, \\`npm start\\`, quick path (Backfill a handful of companies from the Operations page with a cutoff, then Run Daily Check now \u2192 alert), \\`npm run import-data\\` to view the committed snapshot, \\`npm run dev\\`, tests and \\`test:live\\`.\n- News sourcing choice and limitations (ADR-001, ADR-008): ~100-item cap, unofficial feed, redirect links, editions, syndication counted per outlet.\n- LLM section: model and why, invocation (prompt structure, JSON schema output, temperature 0), validation results from #18.\n- Alerts, Mention Status buckets, Coverage Window, review gate.\n- Assumptions, trade-offs, known limitations (incl. TypeScript-on-Node interpretation of \"JavaScript (Node.js)\", no auth, deployment out of scope) and links to ADRs, BACKLOG, CONTEXT, \\`docs/ai-prompts/\\`.\n\n## Acceptance criteria\n- [ ] Someone other than the author follows it on a clean machine without asking questions.\n\n## Depends on\nAll M0\u2013M2 issues, #17, #18.\n\n## Files\n\\`README.md\\`.\nEOF\ncat > 20.md <<EOF\n$H3 \u00b7 ADR-005, ADR-006\n\n## Goal\nThe \\`data/\\` deliverable from a complete real run.\n\n## Scope\n- On a clean database: let the collector import and triage the Seed List; review the Needs Review companies on the companies page.\n- Backfill all active companies with a cutoff of a few days, then **Run Daily Check now** so the snapshot contains a real Alert Digest.\n- Commit the resulting \\`data/\\` export (and \\`data/alerts/\\`), note run date, duration, counts and companies still in Needs Review in \\`data/README.md\\`.\n- Verify \\`npm run import-data\\` on a fresh database reproduces the dashboard.\n\n## Acceptance criteria\n- [ ] \\`data/\\` committed with mentions, sentiment, links, Mention Status per company and at least one Alert Digest.\n- [ ] Import round-trip verified on a clean DB.\n\n## Depends on\nEverything else.\n\n## Files\n\\`data/**\\`.\nEOF\nls | wc -l","description":"Draft issue bodies for UI and delivery milestones"})

### Assistant [2026-10-07T09:47:49.416Z]
I've committed the plan to `main` and created 4 milestones and 19 issues (#2–#20) on GitHub.

**The plan:** [`docs/IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md`](https://github.com/yosefsha/ourcrowd_press_cov/blob/main/docs/IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md) went to `main` as a docs-only commit, together with `CONTEXT.md`, ADR-001 to ADR-010, `BACKLOG.md`, the brief, the seed list and this session's transcript. It contains the architecture diagram, the backend folder layout with the API/collector boundary, the full database schema, every interface and its implementing issue, all config keys, and the complete HTTP API contract.

**Milestones and issues:**

| Milestone | Issues | Can start after |
|---|---|---|
| M0 — Foundation | #2 backend scaffold + docker-compose, #3 frontend scaffold, #4 schema / interfaces / config / empty modules | #2 and #3 now; #4 after #2 |
| M1 — Pipeline & API | #5 Google News, #6 Ollama classifiers, #7 companies + import/triage, #8 run queue + collector + cron, #9 Backfill / Daily Check, #10 alert digests, #11 dashboard read API, #12 data export/import | #4 |
| M2 — Dashboard UI | #13 overview, #14 company detail, #15 companies management, #16 alerts + operations | #3 |
| M3 — Delivery | #17 `npm run setup` / `start`, #18 classifier validation, #19 README, #20 full real run + `data/` | as each issue states |

**What makes them parallel:**
- **#4 lays down every shared piece once:** the full schema migration, every interface, all config keys, and the feature modules already registered. The eight M1 issues then only touch their own folders.
- **The frontend works from the API contract** defined in #3. The UI issues don't have to wait for the backend, and each page lives in its own file.
- **Each issue lists its dependencies and the files it touches**, which is what `/implement-milestone` needs to work out what can run in parallel.

**One thing to watch:** the UI issues (M2) can be built and unit-tested before the backend exists, but they can only be checked end to end once the matching backend issues are merged.

**Next step:** run `/implement-milestone "M0 — Foundation"`. #2 and #3 start in parallel, and #4 follows once #2 is merged. Then M1 and M2 can run side by side.

`plan_grill.txt` (from your `/export`) is still untracked in the repo root, and I left it alone.

### Assistant [2026-10-07T09:57:51.167Z]
I added React Query (TanStack Query) to the plan and to the frontend issues.

- **#3 (frontend scaffold)** now sets it up for the other pages:
  - the provider is installed, with devtools in development builds only;
  - `src/queries.ts` gets one hook per API endpoint: a query hook for every read and a mutation hook for every write;
  - each write refreshes the data it affects. For example, starting a Run refreshes the run list, and editing a company refreshes the company lists and its detail view.
  - Live data refreshes on a timer: Run progress, collector health and unacknowledged alerts.
- **#13–#16 (the pages)** must use those hooks and never fetch data directly.
- **Not in the cache:** UI-only state (Coverage Window, filters, sort, selected company) stays in the URL or the component.
- **`docs/IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md`** describes the same approach next to the API contract. It's committed and pushed to `main`.

I didn't write an ADR, because a library choice like this is easy to change later. Since #3 already sets everything up, the page issues still don't touch each other's files and can run in parallel.

**#2, backend scaffold: [PR #21](https://github.com/yosefsha/ourcrowd_press_cov/pull/21).** I haven't merged it.
- The CI backend job passed and the backend image built.
- The subagent ran the full CI sequence locally from a fresh `npm ci`. Lint, type-check, migrations, 22 unit tests and 6 end-to-end tests all passed, with nothing skipped.
- It also brought up the whole stack in Docker Compose under its own project name, so it didn't touch your shared database: Postgres, then the migration, then the API and the collector. `/health` returned 200. The collector stayed up with no open port and shut down cleanly when stopped. The rule that keeps collector code out of the API caught a deliberate test violation.

**Why the PR's overall checks are red:**
- **The frontend CI job fails** because `frontend/` doesn't exist on that branch yet. #3 adds it.
- **"Claude review" fails** because the repo has no `CLAUDE_CODE_OAUTH_TOKEN` secret. You'd need to run `claude setup-token` and then `gh secret set CLAUDE_CODE_OAUTH_TOKEN --repo yosefsha/ourcrowd_press_cov`.

**Deviations worth knowing:**
- **The automated security review didn't actually check this change.** The `/security-review` skill looked at the main checkout, found no changes, and reviewed nothing. The subagent did the checklist by hand on the branch instead and found no issues. The issue workflow needs that skill to review the branch, so this is worth fixing.
- **Compose host ports can be overridden** (`POSTGRES_HOST_PORT`, `API_HOST_PORT`). This machine already runs a native Postgres on 5432, so a plain `docker compose up` would clash on that port.
- **Migrations live in `src/database/migrations/`**, following the plan, where the NestJS instructions doc says `src/migrations/`.

I'll send the combined report when #3 finishes. #4 can start as soon as you merge #21.

### Assistant [2026-10-07T10:24:09.667Z]
I've added the PR feedback rule to the instructions and pushed it to `main`. The `CLAUDE_CODE_OAUTH_TOKEN` secret still needs you, because the token comes from a browser login I can't do from here.

**The PR feedback rule.** An issue isn't finished when its PR opens. The subagent must then:
1. Wait for CI and the "Claude review" job: `gh pr checks <n> --watch`.
2. Read all feedback: PR comments, review summaries and inline threads.
3. Fix every actionable comment, and every failing check its branch caused, on the same branch, then push to the same PR.
4. Reply to each thread with what changed, or why nothing needs to change.
5. Repeat, since each push triggers a new review, until no new actionable feedback is left. That's capped at 3 rounds, and anything still open goes in its final report.

Failures the branch didn't cause, like a missing secret, get reported instead of worked around. The rule is in `CLAUDE.md` and in the `implement-issue` skill as a new "Step 5b". The skill's final report now also includes the CI and review status and the comments addressed. I also sent the rule to the subagent already working on #4, so it applies there too.

**To set the secret, run these two commands in this prompt:**

```
! claude setup-token
! gh secret set CLAUDE_CODE_OAUTH_TOKEN --repo yosefsha/ourcrowd_press_cov
```

The first opens the browser login and prints a token. The second prompts you to paste it. `setup-token` needs a Claude Pro or Max subscription. Once the secret is set, re-run the "Claude review" check on PR #22 from the Actions tab, or push to the branch, so the review runs.

### Assistant [2026-10-07T10:42:39.829Z]
**#4, schema / interfaces / config: [PR #26](https://github.com/yosefsha/ourcrowd_press_cov/pull/26) passes CI. One decision from you is needed before it's merged.**
- All four checks and the Claude review pass. It took two rounds: the first push was missing the `src/coverage/` module because a too-broad `.gitignore` rule excluded it. The subagent fixed that on the same PR.
- **Tests:** 418 unit and 44 end-to-end, none skipped. The seed-list parser is tested against all 257 lines with exact expected results.
- **Migrations:** run against its own Postgres, including running, reverting and re-running. A check confirmed the migration matches the code with no gaps.
- **Separation:** collector code is kept out of the API, checked both when the code is built and at runtime.
- **API shapes:** the backend uses exactly the data shapes the frontend chose in #22.

**The decision: what's the Mention Status of a company last mentioned more than 90 days ago?**
The buckets stop at Quiet (31–90 days), so the subagent made over-90 count as **No coverage**. That contradicts our glossary, where No coverage means *no Mention found since collection began*. The case comes up over time: a company covered once in June has been silent for 4 months by October.
- **Recommended:** Quiet becomes "more than 30 days" with no upper limit, and No coverage strictly means no Mention ever. Buckets keep their meaning, and the exact "last mentioned 120 days ago" text still tells the full story.
- **Alternative:** add a fifth bucket, "Dormant" (> 90 days). It's more precise, but it's one more state everywhere (types, badges, filters).

**Other choices it made (in the PR body; I'm fine with all of them):**
- **The news-source interface also reports whether a search hit Google's result cap,** which the "100+" label needs.
- **Run types and alert channels are registered as lists.** Adding one never means editing the worker.
- **The error "stage" is stored as plain text,** so adding a stage needs no migration.

**Handled:** the seed list and `data/` folder paths don't exist inside the container. The #17 subagent is already editing `docker-compose.yml`, so I've asked it to mount both, keeping `data/` committed since it's a deliverable.

**After you decide and merge #26, I'll fan out M1, all eight backend issues (#5–#12), in parallel.**

**Ready to merge now:** #23 (company detail) and #27 (alerts and operations).
**Still running:** #13, #15 and #17.

So: Quiet with no upper limit, or a Dormant bucket?

No args were given and the primary checkout is on `main`, so I picked the target from the worktree path that appeared in the skill text. Tell me if you meant `feat/15-companies-admin`.

This branch is mostly groundwork. It adds pure domain types, port interfaces with their error classes, TypeORM entities, the `CreateSchema` migration, empty Nest feature modules, and new env config with validation. No controllers, adapters, HTTP clients or SQL from user input are included yet.

---

```
[LOW] SSRF / unsafe URLs — backend/src/domain/article.ts:7-11, backend/src/domain/alert-digest.ts:9-10
FoundArticle.outletUrl / googleUrl / publisherUrl and AlertMention.url are typed as plain strings, and
the articles table stores them as text with no check. These URLs come from an external feed (Google
News) and will later be shown as links on the dashboard and in alert channels. Nothing here exploits
that yet; the risk lands with the NewsSource adapter and the frontend.
Fix: when the NewsSource adapter is built, keep only http(s) URLs (drop `javascript:`, `data:` and
others). Also check the scheme again when rendering. The collector must not fetch publisherUrl
server-side without limiting the scheme and host.
```

```
[LOW] Debug/dev defaults — backend/src/config/validation.ts:24 (DEFAULT_DATABASE_URL), doc comment ~L77-80
DATABASE_URL still defaults to `postgresql://app:app@localhost:5432/app`. That default was already on
main, but this branch now says the defaults are "the production values", with DATABASE_URL as the one
exception. So a production deploy that forgets DATABASE_URL quietly falls back to weak local
credentials instead of failing at boot.
Fix (optional, in line with the docs): when NODE_ENV=production, require DATABASE_URL and allow no
default. Or keep it as is and accept it as a localhost-only tool.
```

```
[LOW] Sensitive data in error objects — backend/src/classification/classifier-errors.ts:14-16
ClassifierOutputInvalid stores the raw LLM output "for diagnosis". That output may repeat article
content. It is not a secret or PII today, but whoever logs this error later should cap its length.
Fix: when logging, cut `output` down (e.g. to 500 chars) so huge or odd model output doesn't fill the logs.
```

---

- No issues: Secrets / credentials. Test DB URLs in `configuration.spec.ts` are dummy fixtures, and every value is read from `process.env` only inside `configuration()`.
- No issues: SQL injection. The migration uses constant DDL only. The `${table}` / `${type}` interpolations in `down()` loop over hardcoded literal arrays.
- No issues: Shell injection. There is no `child_process`. The `isCronExpression` validator is strict, and a test rejects `'0 7 * * *; rm -rf /'`.
- No issues: Sensitive data in logs. No logging was added. The `InvalidEnvironmentError` messages are fixed strings and never repeat env values, so the DB password can't leak at boot.
- No issues: Auth gaps. `ApiModule` registers Companies, Runs, Coverage and Alerts, but they are empty `@Module({})` shells with no controllers. The project has no auth on purpose (`docs/BACKLOG.md`: "a local tool on `localhost`"). Once admin and run-trigger controllers land (e.g. issue 15), the API has to stay bound to localhost until the session-auth backlog item is done.
- No issues: Debug/dev backdoors. No `DEBUG`, `verify=False` or commented-out checks.
- No issues: Insecure deserialization. No `eval`, `pickle` or `yaml.load`. The `jsonb` columns (`params`, `progress`) are written by our own code.
- No issues: Cloud/IAM least privilege. No IaC in scope.
- No issues: New dependencies. `package.json` and the lockfile are unchanged.

Outside security, the schema looks sound. It has unique and check constraints (one active Run, live display names unique ignoring case, a single-row heartbeat), and its FKs mostly use `RESTRICT`, with `CASCADE` only on the error and digest-item child rows.

| Severity | Count |
|----------|-------|
| CRITICAL | 0 |
| HIGH     | 0 |
| MEDIUM   | 0 |
| LOW      | 3 |

Nothing at CRITICAL or HIGH, so security is no reason to hold the PR.</result>

Before the fix, every run saw nothing. The argument still isn't recognised cleanly, though: it said "No args were given", then found the worktree path in its own instructions anyway. M1 can rely on it.

**Results for #26:** no critical, high or medium findings. There were 3 low ones:
- **News URLs aren't checked.** Links from the news feed are stored as plain text. The Google News issue (#5) should keep only `http(s)` links and must never fetch a publisher URL from the server without restrictions. I'll put that in #5's instructions when M1 starts. The frontend already checks links when it shows them.
- **`DATABASE_URL` has a weak local default.** It falls back to `app:app@localhost`, so a production deploy that forgets to set it would quietly use those credentials. Deployment is out of scope, so this is fine for a localhost tool. Requiring the variable when `NODE_ENV=production` would be a cheap hardening if you want it.
- **Raw model output in errors.** The classifier's "invalid output" error keeps the full model output. It should be cut down when logged; I'll pass that to #6.

Still running: the #25 conflict fix and the #26 Quiet change. I'll report when both PRs are green and ready for you to merge.

I've told #8 to start without #9's code being present (Runs of an unknown type just fail with a clear message), so #8 can merge on its own. ### Assistant [2026-10-07T15:31:42.175Z]
The usage limit has reset, so I've resumed all eight M1 subagents. Each one checks its worktree's commits first and carries on from there, without redoing finished work.

**Coordination changes I passed on while resuming:**
- **Ollama health has one source.** #6 provides a single shared Ollama health check that the other modules can use. The collector then refuses to start without Ollama, with a clear "`ollama pull qwen2.5:7b`" message. I told #8 to use #6's check in its heartbeat instead of building its own.
- **CI has no Ollama**, so once #6 merges, any test that starts the whole collector fails unless it stubs that health check. I told #7, #8, #9 and #10 to use the same pattern in their tests (stub the health check, and use in-memory versions of the classifiers).
- **#7 now has the revised merge order**: it was the one subagent I hadn't reached before the cutoff.

**M1 merge order:**
1. #5, #6, #10 and #12 (and #11, which doesn't depend on the collector code), in any order.
2. #8.
3. #7.
4. #9 last.

**Expect some red CI along the way:** #7's and #9's tests that start the whole collector will fail until the PRs before them are merged. Each will say so in its PR body, then merge `main` and go green before its final push.

**Still worth doing on your side:** install Ollama (`brew install ollama`, then `ollama pull qwen2.5:7b`) so #6 can record real verdicts. #18 and #20 also need it.

I'll report as the PRs come in.

### Assistant [2026-10-07T20:35:05.137Z]
**#10, Alert Digests: [PR #29](https://github.com/yosefsha/ourcrowd_press_cov/pull/29) is ready for you to merge.**
- All checks pass, and the Claude review is clean on round 2. In round 1 it found that the alerts list had no limit; that's fixed with a limit that defaults to 50 and is capped at 200.
- **What it does:**
  - Builds one Alert Digest per Daily Check from that Run's New Mentions, grouped by company with negatives first. A Run with none gets no digest, only a log line, and building the same Run twice notifies only once.
  - Delivers it to the log and to `data/alerts/<date>-run<id>.json`.
  - Adds the list, detail and acknowledge endpoints, matching the frontend contract exactly.
- **Tests:** 445 unit and 59 end-to-end on its own Postgres, using articles recorded from real Google News. The tests include the 7-day staleness rule and the case where a Mention confirmed in a later Run still alerts.

**Security finding to decide on (medium):** none of the API's endpoints that change state check where a request came from. A malicious website could make your browser call `localhost` to acknowledge a digest, start a Run or edit a company. The design called for rejecting requests with a mismatched `Origin` header, but I only recorded it with the deferred auth item in the backlog. It's a few lines in the shared API setup, so it belongs in a small follow-up issue of its own. **Do you want me to create it** and fold it into M1, or leave it in the backlog with auth?

**Status:**
- **Ready to merge:** #29. It touches no shared files, so it can go in now.
- **PR open, review pending:** #30 (#11).
- **Running:** #5, #6, #12 and #8.
- **Still to resume:** #7 and #9.

### Assistant [2026-10-07T20:35:56.253Z]
**#6, Ollama classifiers: [PR #31](https://github.com/yosefsha/ourcrowd_press_cov/pull/31) is ready for you to merge.** All checks pass, and the Claude review found nothing.
- **What it does:**
  - **Ollama client:** answers are forced into a JSON format, temperature is 0, and only a limited number of requests run at once.
  - **Three classifiers:** relevance, sentiment toward the company, and ambiguity triage. Their answers are checked again on our side, and a malformed answer is retried once.
  - **Prompts:** versioned, with hard-case examples (an unrelated Harvey, a layoffs story), Hebrew input accepted, and an instruction to treat article text as data, not commands.
  - **Startup:** the built collector exits with an exact message ("Start Ollama… run `ollama pull qwen2.5:7b`") when Ollama is missing. The API starts fine without it.
- **Tests:** 501 unit and 44 end-to-end on its own Postgres.
- **Security:** 0 critical, high or medium findings; 3 low. Two were fixed, including truncating raw model output and removing control characters from it. The third: the UI must show the classifier's `reason` as escaped text. React escapes text by default, so this holds as long as no component inserts raw HTML.

**Shared test files it changed:** it added a test helper that starts the collector without Ollama, and updated the two existing tests that start the collector to use it. ### Assistant [2026-10-07T20:37:43.573Z]
**#5, Google News: [PR #32](https://github.com/yosefsha/ourcrowd_press_cov/pull/32) is ready for you to merge.** All checks pass, the Claude review had "no actionable findings", and the live test passed against real Google News (3 of 3).
- **Search:**
  - Uses curated search terms or the quoted name and aliases.
  - Covers both News Editions (English and Hebrew).
  - Filters results to the exact date window.
  - Reports "capped" when Google returns 100 items.
- **Parsing:**
  - Extracts the Google article ID.
  - Cleans titles and Outlet names to plain text.
  - Fails loudly if Google's feed format changes.
- **Security findings from earlier, all handled:**
  - Only http(s) links are kept.
  - The server only ever contacts `https://news.google.com` and rejects redirects, so feed URLs are stored but never fetched.
  - Timeouts and size limits on every response.
  - Requests are spaced at least 300 ms apart.
- **Test fixtures recorded from the real feed:** Stripe (capped at 100), Harvey (ambiguous), Island and ZutaCore in Hebrew, and one empty result. Other modules can reuse them as a recorded news source in their tests.
- **Shared files:** it adds the `fast-xml-parser` dependency, plus the `test:live` and fixture-recording scripts.

**Cost to know about:** resolving the real publisher link costs 2 throttled requests per article, which makes a full Backfill noticeably slower. I've told #9 not to resolve the same article twice.

### Assistant [2026-10-08T05:29:32.853Z]
It is a queue, just an in-process one. `ConcurrencyLimiter` (`backend/src/classification/ollama/concurrency-limiter.ts`) is a small semaphore: at most `OLLAMA_NUM_PARALLEL` requests run at once, and any extra caller waits in a first-in-first-out list until a slot frees up. What it isn't is a separate queue service (Redis/BullMQ, SQS, pg-boss).

**Why a limiter and not a separate queue service:**

1. **The job queue already exists, one level up.** ADR-009 queues the Run in the `runs` table, and the collector claims one at a time. Classification calls are small steps *inside* that Run, each finishing in seconds. Queuing them again would be a queue inside a queue.
2. **The caller needs the answer right away to decide its next step.** Sentiment only runs if relevance says yes, and the Run's failure counter needs each call's outcome. With a separate queue you'd push a job, then wait for and match up its result. That's more code for the same flow.
3. **Recovery is already handled by the database.** If the collector crashes mid-Run, unclassified Candidates stay `pending` in Postgres and the next Run picks them up. A queue would add a second place to keep in sync.
4. **There's one Ollama, on the same machine.** The only reason to limit anything is Ollama's own parallelism (`OLLAMA_NUM_PARALLEL`). Above that, Ollama queues requests itself. The limiter makes callers wait *on our side*, so a request's 120 s timeout only starts once it's actually sent. Otherwise requests would time out while sitting in Ollama's queue.
5. **No extra infrastructure.** A queue service means a new container in Compose and CI, which the repo rules say not to add unless needed.

**When a separate queue *would* be right:** several collector replicas sharing one Ollama (the cap would then have to be shared across processes), or work spread across several GPU machines. At that point the classifier interface stays the same and you'd swap the implementation behind it, which is what the interfaces are for.

**While checking this, I found a real gap.** #9's pipeline classifies Candidates **one at a time**: `company-collection.service.ts:154` is a plain `for … await` loop. So the limiter never has more than one request in flight, and `OLLAMA_NUM_PARALLEL=2` gives no speed-up. My Backfill estimate (1.5–3 hours) assumed 2 at once, so as written a full Backfill takes longer.

The fix belongs in #9: classify a company's pending Candidates up to `numParallel` at a time, sending them through the classifier so the limiter controls them. It has to keep two existing rules intact:
- the consecutive-failure counter that stops a Run after 5 Ollama failures;
- the `confirmed_in_run_id` stamping that alerts rely on.

Shall I have the #9 subagent make that change while it waits for its final merge from `main`?
